ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u31	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Culture and Media about the draft law presented by the President of the Republic of Poland, Mr Andrzej Duda on the preparation and implementation of investments in the reconstruction of the Saski Palace, the Brühl Palace and the tenements at ul. Royal in Warsaw, prints no. 1388 and no. 1407. Marshal of the Sejm according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 On 8 July 2021, 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm addressed the above draft law to the Committee on Culture and Media for First Reading. The Committee on Culture and Media of the Republic of Poland, after first reading and considering this draft law at its meeting on 20 July 2021, brings it to the High Sejm of the Republic of Poland to approve the attached draft law. During the meeting of the Committee on Culture and Media, we heard information about the bill presented by the Minister, the representative of the President. During the discussion, the Minister, the General Monument Preserver, also the Deputy President of the City of Warsaw, representatives of the Saski Association 2018. There were also representatives of the Office of the Programme, as well as representatives of the Mazowieckie Voivodeship and Members - members of the Committee on Culture and Media, as well as from outside the committee - who asked many questions. There has been a few hours of discussion about this project, which has aroused the keen interest of ladies and gentlemen. During the discussion a number of themes were discussed, including: The idea of a special act and the participation of the city of Warsaw in this project were referred to. Because for several decades there has been no reconstruction of the Saski Palace, the Brühl Palace and the tenements at ul. Royal in Warsaw, the committee decided that this law was necessary in order to restore these very important buildings for the city of Warsaw and for Poland. The discussion also raised the question of the need to intervene in a section of the Saski Park greenery. Here the argument is that on the one hand actually, in order to be able to rebuild all the houses on the street. As well as the Brühl Palace, there is a need for some interference in greenery, but with the current possibilities it is not a problem to move, to overstep some trees, as well as new plantings, or to establish new green fragments. Many times there was also a thread in the discussion concerning the habit of the present shape of the Tomb of the Unknown Soldier. However, it was argued that the tomb of the Unknown Soldier was created in the period of the independent Second Republic of Poland and was as much within the appearance of the entire Saski Palace at that time. He referred to the struggle for Poland's independence during the First World War and the Polish-Bolshevik War and other fights of the period of regaining independence - this tradition will be established in the reconstruction of the Saski Palace and the context of the Unknown Soldier's tomb will be fully restored. The subject matter of the special purpose vehicle and the remuneration associated with it were also raised, but by comparison it was considered that these earnings, even if they reached the upper limit, would not be higher than in some municipal companies. Communication issues were also raised. The reference is that they are planned, among others. underground parking lots, so that it is possible to provide efficient communication solutions. It is also important that after the reconstruction of the Saski Palace, the Brühl Palace and the tenements at ul. It will also be possible to move the seat of the Mazowieckie Voivodeship here. In turn, there will be more room for the president of the city. Warsaw, so it will take place for the benefit of both parties. During the detailed work on the Law, to which we went after a few hours of general debate, one major amendment was introduced: the Council of Reconstruction was strengthened. The Council is to be chaired by a representative of the President of the Republic of Poland, a representative of the President of the Council of Ministers, two representatives of the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage, representatives of the Minister of National Defence, the President of the Republic of Poland. Warsaw and the general conservator of monuments. We have also added a representative of the City Council of Warsaw and - what is important in the context of the place where we are and where we are sitting - a representative of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland. The work took many hours, some Members had to go late to meetings of other committees, so attendance at the end of the meeting was a little less. In the final vote, 12 votes in favour, with two votes against and four abstentions, the Committee on Culture and Media recommended that the High House adopt this law. It is worth adding some historical themes about the idea of rebuilding the Saski Palace, the Brühl Palace and the tenements at ul. Royal. First of all, it is the completion of the reconstruction of the most important buildings, buildings of Warsaw which were methodically demolished, plannedly and fiercely by the German occupier. This reconstruction has been proposed since 1946, so it is high time to take up this task. It will also recreate an important part of the large-scale urban concept of this part of the capital, called Axis Saska, the closure of the square of Marshal Józef Piłsudski - these buildings will complete this square. The important thing is that the Saski Palace was the headquarters of the General Staff and later of the Polish Army General Staff in the period of the independent Second Republic. Here he played Polish Army already in 1918 General Tadeusz Jordan Rozwadowski, later among others. among the chiefs of chief of staff was Marshal Józef Piłsudski. Also here there was a branch of the Second Polish Army Staff, Polish intelligence, here during the war against the Bolsheviks por. drip. Jan Kowalewski broke with his collaborators, outstanding Polish mathematicians: Sierpiński, Mazurkiewicz, Leśniewski, Bolshevik ciphers. This law leaves forks, indicates how the base of dimension is calculated and indicates multiplier forks - from one to fifteen times. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u161	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report by the Committee on Education, Science and Youth on the Government's draft law amending the Law - Law on Higher Education and Science and the Law - Provisions introducing the Law - Law on Higher Education and Science, contained in print No. 1345. The report is contained in print No 1375. The government project entered the Sejm on 28 June, and on 7 July the first reading was held in the Committee on Education, Science and Youth. The reasons for the bill were presented by Mr Wojciech Murdzek, State Secretary at the Ministry of Education and Science. He drew attention to the important role of vocational universities in the academic map of Poland and the considerable impact on regional development, especially visible in smaller urban centres. Most state higher vocational schools are located in cities that were voivodships in 1975. Undoubtedly, the success of these universities could be greater if not for a very happy name, i.e. a higher vocational school. A broad consultation with the scientific community of state higher vocational schools and local authorities indicated the need to change the name of these universities. The Ministry of Education and Science proposed the name "practical academy' to correct this image problem and to point out to secondary school graduates that these universities are academics. Minister Wojciech Murdzek also pointed out the criteria contained in the project on which the possibility of renaming depends. The following conditions should be met jointly: operate for a minimum of 10 years, have a minimum number of students - 250, of which at least 100 in fixed education, a minimum of 50% of academic teachers should be employed in that university as a basic place of work, have first or second degree studies or have a uniform master's degree in at least five courses, and provide training in at least one course of studies preparing for the pursuit of the professions referred to in Article. Article 68 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1, or the course of studies ending with an engineering degree or an engineer master's degree. Let me just remind you that in the article. Article 68 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 we have such professions as the profession of doctor, dentist, pharmacist, nurse, midwife, laboratory diagnostics, physiotherapist, paramedic, veterinarian, architect or teacher. Education standards shall be taken into account in study programmes in these fields. Last condition: no course of studies conducted by the university can be assessed negatively as a result of the Polish Accreditation Commission's assessment of the quality of education. In addition, the project contains provisions on the flexibility of teacher education. The professional university will be able to obtain permission to conduct preparatory studies for the profession of teacher without having to conclude an agreement with a university with a minimum level of B+ in the discipline to which the course of those studies is assigned. For this to happen, the university will have to meet the conditions for changing the name I was just talking about. I stress that she will have to meet all these conditions together. The draft law also addresses the problem of excessive financial burden on doctoral institutions. The current rules allow PhD students in doctoral school to work for research and grant projects and at the same time oblige them to pay a doctoral scholarship. The amendment excludes the right to receive a doctoral scholarship of at least 150% by doctoral students. the amount of the doctoral scholarship in connection with the implementation of grants. The proposed solution will eliminate the double burden on the PhD student and reduce the gap between the costs of the PhD student groups. In the course of the work of the Committee on Education, Science and Youth, parts of the academic community have emerged, supported by some of the opposition Members, calling into question the validity of the proposed changes. The opinion was that allowing the name to be changed to the name "practical academy" and the flexibility of teacher education would lead to a reduction in the level of teaching. With these voices they polemicized, among others. Minister Wojciech Murdzek and President of the Conference of Rectors of State Higher Vocational Schools Prof. Dariusz Słowik. The latter, Prof. Rawik, stated that it is not true to claim that teacher education in state higher vocational schools is carried out at a lower level than in other types of university. At the same time, he pointed out that there are more practical activities in the WWSZ, which are so important in teacher education, among others. an obligation of 6 months of practice which does not exist in other such universities. On the other hand, the only institution authorised to assess the level of education is the Polish Accreditation Commission, which positively evaluates state higher vocational schools. He also pointed out that teaching courses are standardized courses, regardless of the type of university, strict requirements must be met. Professor Rawik, on behalf of the Conference of Rectors of State Higher Vocational Schools, gave a very positive assessment of the possibility of changing the name of the project, but he proposed that instead of the name "practical academy," the name "academy of applied sciences." This text was corrected by Parliamentary Club Law and Justice and received the support of a majority of Members, members of the Education, Science and Youth Committee. The Commission rejected the amendments tabled by the Civic Coalition and adopted several editorial and legislative amendments. In conclusion, the Committee on Education, Science and Youth recommends the adoption by the High Sejm of a draft law contained in print no. 1375. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[Piotr Zgorzelski, Vice-Marshal of the Sejm, is the chairman of the proceedings.]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u216	Thank you very much, sir. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u217	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen! I am very pleased to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice on the Law on amending the Law on Maritime Labour. Ladies and gentlemen! In short, this bill improves the comfort and safety of seafarers' work. This law is the consequence of international agreements and amendments to those agreements. Of course, Poland joined these agreements in 2015. This law is also based on some practical comments regarding the pursuit of the profession of seafarer, which have already taken place over the last few years. This law concerns inter alia. the piracy problem. It protects seafarers by increasing the continuity of the employment contract and wages also for the time of possible detention by the kidnappers until repatriating to the place that the sailor wishes. This is a big and very important change. This law also changes the settlement time from 6 months to 1 month. It also includes changes in the recognition and issuing of health certificates, as well as in the issuing of sailing books and entries to them. These changes, in turn, result in considerable flexibility in terms of the possibility of concluding various contracts, the possibility of taking up work. This makes it easier for all the procedural and administrative activities that are related to health certificates and sailing books. Finally, it is worth mentioning that this law aligns the situation of crews, seafarers working on conventional ships - of course conventional according to the understanding of certain laws - with those who are not included in these concepts, i.e. working on tugs, floating cranes, dredgers and other specialised units. In our opinion, it is this is a much-needed change, a much-needed law, which can actually be very useful for seafarers in their extremely difficult work. Ladies and gentlemen! Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of this bill. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u218	Thank you very much, sir. I now ask Mr Arkadiusz Carrot to present the position of the Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u219	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! The draft law on amending the Maritime Labour Act is primarily intended to improve the workers' rights of seafarers whose ships have been attacked. In general, it must be said that these issues which are raised in this project concern the implementation of the amendments to the Polish law resulting from the amendments to the Maritime Labour Convention. I would like to stress and draw attention to the fact that the changes that have been proposed in the project concern, inter alia, the following: extending the validity of maritime employment contracts for that period which is linked to the possible detention of seafarers on board as a result of acts of piracy or armed assault on a ship. Moreover, the project assumes that the ship owner is obliged to pay in the event of holding the seafarer with adequate remuneration and benefits resulting from a maritime employment contract. It is worth noting that the bill also provides for an extension of the rights that a seafarer obtains to be repatriated free of charge when a ship is attacked. It should be noted that the proposed law amends the law that governs and clarifies it, which is currently in force. It is worth noting that a certain change of definition is being made in the case of shipowners, seafarers or so-called. a non-conventional vessel, but a change is also made which concerns the changeover from the euro to the gold, with fees for the issuing of sailing books by the directors of maritime offices. I think these are issues that are not controversial in any way. It should be said that they are needed and will in these difficult and often very bad situations serve seafarers who should be paid in situations that are not dependent on them. Since we are talking about maritime work and you are a minister, I would like to remind you that maritime work issues are not just issues arising from this law. Recently, at the meeting of the Maritime Affairs Committee, we have discussed the situation of people who also work at sea. We were talking about the Marine Search and Rescue Service, and we need to say from this stand in the Sejm that their situation is getting worse. Our great concern is that maritime search and rescue workers, those who care about the safety of people at sea every day, receive less money because their salaries have been reduced. Of course, there was a fund that was taken away, but it is a clear deterioration of the working conditions that those responsible for security have so far had. From this information it appears that the Minister of Finance does not want to agree to such matters, and I think that it is from this House that a clear and loud voice should come from the Sejm that the situation of these people must be improved, that the safety of people at sea cannot be spared. I am convinced that these are the actions that are needed, and when we talk about work at sea, we should also pay attention to this. Although this issue may not be directly enshrined in this bill, we must remember these people. In conclusion, I would like to point out that those issues which have been included in the draft law, which concern the implementation of these amendments, which result from the amendments to the Maritime Labour Convention, are changes that are needed and will serve seafarers. Let's hope they never have to use them, because there will be no such thing as assault on the ship and piracy. On behalf of the Civic Coalition Club, I declare that this bill will be supported by us. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u221	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! We are indeed debating a draft amendment to the Maritime Labour Act. Laws are very important, especially for those regions of Poland, which lie by the sea, which on a daily basis deal with this type of work and have many inhabitants who work on ships. Generally, the direction of this law is good, but this is the minimum that could be introduced in this law, because the truth is that we thought that indeed the Ministry and the government would start to work seriously on the law, which, first of all, will cause Polish ships to sail under the Polish flag. Unfortunately, today you can say that actually 4% or 5% The entire Polish fleet sails under the Polish flag, because the rest is registered somewhere in Panama, the Bahamas or the famous San Escobar, and it is in my opinion and in our opinion the whole problem that this law should solve. Undoubtedly, the MLC Convention is being implemented by this law, and those provisions that are there in this Convention have emerged. Indeed, it improves the situation of seafarers, but does not solve all the problems that we are talking about here, connected, for example, with the Polish flag and with the Polish law, which would apply, also as regards the labour matters of Polish seafarers. Of course, the provisions of this law concerning the right to a pension in the event of a loss of health go in the right direction. Of course, this is the protection of workers' rights with regard to piracy, which is now actually happening worldwide. It would seem that these cases would be less during the pandemic period, but it turns out that there were more than one year before the pandemic. Therefore, this is a good direction, the Act also defines issues related to the ship owner and defining who is the sailor, who is the person, who is the employee. The Act does not solve issues relating to the social security of seafarers, because this is an eternal problem that has existed for many, many years. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u241	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u242	Thank you very much. I ask Katarzyna Ueberhan, Left, to present the position of the club.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u243	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Left Club on the Senate draft law amending the Law on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters, Prints No. 994 and 1309 containing the draft amendment of the Act and the report of the commission following work in committee at first reading. Everyone shall have the right to a fair and public hearing without undue delay by a competent, independent, impartial and independent court. This is the case with Art. Article 45 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The law must not close anybody's judicial path to the investigation of violated freedoms or rights, as provided for in Article. Article 77 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. These important constitutional guarantees, the principles and rights of the citizen to the court are now in breach of part of the Article. 104a Law of 28 July 2005 on judicial costs in civil matters. In accordance with the judgment of the Constitutional Court of 29 April 2020, that provision is incompatible with the Constitution, it infringes the aforementioned provisions of the Basic Law insofar as in electronic proceedings it excludes the possibility of the defendant to obtain an exemption from judicial costs. The purpose of the amendments introduced in this submission is to adapt the Polish law system to the said judgment of the Court. The proposed proposal amends the Law of 28 July 2005 on judicial costs in civil matters by adding after Article. Article 98a 98b providing that expenditure shall be borne by the State Treasury in electronic proceedings. In the course of the committee's work, an article was also added. 2 extending the application of the new Article. 98b in such a way that it is covered by cases already initiated and not completed before the date of entry into force of this Law. The amendments adapt the Polish right to the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal in the following areas. In the electronic reminder proceedings, the defendant's possibility of obtaining an exemption from judicial costs was excluded and in this respect the Act on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters of 2005 was declared incompatible with the Constitution. The Constitutional Tribunal took the view that, where an obligation to pay court costs is imposed and there is a state obligation to pay them, there should also be an effective system of exemptions from those charges for the balance of the parties to proceedings in order to ensure the right of access to the court. In the new article. 98b is proposed to use the formula used in Article. 98a, as a result of which the exemption from the obligation to bear expenditure in electronic reproving proceedings will now be of a statutory nature. The proposed changes will be relatively rarely applied, as due to the nature of the renouncing procedure, expenditure will also be incidental in the course of this procedure. However, given that these amendments align the system of law with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, but also guarantee the fuller exercise of the citizen's right to court, the Left Club supports these arrangements and the draft proposal submitted to the Senate to amend the Law on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u244	Thank you very much. I now ask Mr Krzysztof Paszyk to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Coalition Poland.

As underlined in the explanatory memorandum of the draft, the Constitutional Tribunal took the view that, if an obligation to pay legal costs is imposed, there is a state obligation to pay them, there should also be an effective system of exemptions from those charges for the balance of the parties to proceedings in order to ensure the right of access to the court. In accordance with the draft law, an additional provision will be added to the Law on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters, which provides that expenditure is borne by the State Treasury in electronic proceedings. In the new article. 98b It is therefore proposed to adopt a solution whereby the exemption from the obligation to bear expenditure in the case of electronic renouncing proceedings will be of a statutory nature. This solution, due to its automation, will not further involve the resources of the judiciary in assessing whether the property situation of the parties allows for exemption from judicial costs. This will improve such proceedings and eliminate situations which would restrict the right to court. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u256	Thank you very much. The list of persons enrolled in the questions has been exhausted. Now I ask Catherine Frydrych, Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Justice, to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u257	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law amends the Law on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters. It consists of the addition of an article. 98b. As already mentioned, the draft law implements the ruling of the Constitutional Tribunal of 29 April 2020 in case P 19/16. In that judgment, the Constitutional Tribunal held that the provision of the Article was unconstitutional. 104a of the Law on Judicial Costs in Civil Matters to the extent that in electronic proceedings, that provision excludes the possibility for the defendant to obtain an exemption from judicial costs. I would like to point out that the proposed provision is: in electronic proceedings, the State Treasury is responsible for the expenditure. Expenditure is a slightly narrower concept than judicial costs. The judgment was delivered on the basis of a case in which expenditure on the expert's opinion had to be paid. In view of the rare occurrence of such situations and in order to maintain the efficiency of the proceedings, it is proposed that both parties be legally exempted from expenditure in these proceedings. The project assumes that the State Treasury will bear these expenses definitively, which means that the parties will not be burdened with them. In the course of the work, an article was added. 2 relating to the regulation relating to what is happening in the case of cases initiated and not completed before the date of entry into force of this Law. There is no doubt that the regulation that the proposed provision also applies to these situations is correct, but justifies the application of a law which is more favourable to the parties in all pending and unfinished cases. The government has a positive opinion on the draft submitted. On the other hand, with regard to issues that were raised during the course of the questions, I would like to point out that it is not so that the right to court is limited or dependent on the value of the citizen's wallet. The citizen has the right to apply for exemption from judicial costs at any stage of the proceedings. The Court of First Instance shall then examine whether the conditions for the party not to pay those costs in whole or in part, including in respect of expenditure, are met. This regulation guarantees the possibility for each party to make such an application and the court considers whether the application is justified or whether, in fact, the financial situation of the party does not allow it to bear legal costs. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u259	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u260	Thank you very much. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would listen to the five-minute statements on behalf of the clubs and three-minute statements on behalf of the circles. I'm opening a discussion. I would like to ask Mrs Iwona Arent to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u261	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the report of the Committee on Constitutional Responsibility on the draft law amending the Law on the Court of State, prints Nos 993 and 1330. This is a bill prepared by the Senate and addressed by the Senate resolution to the Marshal on 13 January 2021. This is a very short amendment to the Law on the Court of State, which contains only two articles, and it is very short. The amendment proposes to amend the body empowered to issue the Rules of Procedure of the Court of State. Until now, under the Law on the Court of State, the Sejm was the body which established the Rules of Procedure of the Court of State. Following the adoption of this amendment, the Tribunal itself will issue its rules of procedure and this will be regarded as an act of an internal nature, and very well. This project is the consequence of a petition which has been submitted to the Senate. In this petition, the applicant proposed to amend the provision of the Article. 27. She proposed to be replaced by the following: the competent authority for issuing the Rules of Procedure of the Court of State shall be the President of the Court of State. Of course, the previous provision, which said that the Sejm adopted the Rules of Procedure of the Court of State, was issued in the times of the Polish People's Republic, on 6 July 1982, pursuant to the resolution of the Polish Sejm of the People's Republic. When the Law on the Court of State was passed in 1982, there was no clear distinction between the sources of universal law and internal acts. Following the adoption of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland in 1997, public authorities may issue statutory normative acts concerning another body only in strictly defined situations. Firstly, it is either an organisationally subordinate body or has been authorised to issue an implementing act on the basis of a statutory authorisation, e.g. regulation, and the Sejm does not have the possibility to issue regulations. It therefore seems logical that these Rules of Procedure of the Sejm should not establish that the Court of State should itself lay down the rules of procedure, all the more so as the Court of State is not subject to the rules of procedure of the Sejm. It is therefore appropriate that the Tribunal itself establish these rules of procedure. That is why this amendment is very well founded. I propose to adapt the content of the article. 27 to the current system of sources of law by authorising the Court of State to amend its Rules of Procedure by an absolute majority in the presence of at least half of the statutory number of members of the Court of State. The petitioner's request was that the Rules of Procedure be prepared by the President of the Court of State, while the amendment we have been working on is the solution that the Court itself will do so by an absolute majority in the presence of at least half of the statutory number of members of the Court of State. Following the entry into force of this amendment, the previous rules will cease to apply, a new regulation will be adopted which will have a broader scope. Finally, I would like to refer briefly to the State Court Act in general. It was passed even in the time of the Polish People's Republic, in 1982. To date, this law has not been amended or the rules of the Court of State and constitutional responsibility in Poland have not been amended. Of course, there were minor cosmetic changes just like the one we are working on today, and in the meantime the constitution has also changed, so it would be worth considering the preparation of a new comprehensive law on the Court of State, possibly on the direction of constitutional liability in Poland. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

High Seym! The previous record of this article was - here I quote: The Sejm adopts the Rules of Procedure of the Court of State. Since the Court of State is not an organisational unit subordinate to the Sejm, this excludes the possibility of issuing internal acts before the Court of State. Nor does the Sejm have a statutory authority to issue regulations in this respect. Moreover, the Constitution of the Republic of Poland does not provide for the possibility for the Sejm to issue regulations at all. In view of the above, the Senate's problem has given the petition P9/52/19 to the Senate the dimension of the statutory amendments. During the consultation on this project, the Supreme Court and its first President, who are, as we know, at the same time the President of the Court of State, who noted that the proposed regulation corresponds to the general principle of the autonomy of the courts and tribunals, namely the Article. 173 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland, which cannot be said to be the norm resulting from the current wording of this article. 27 Law on the Court of State according to which the Rules of Procedure of the Court of State are adopted by the Sejm. We proceed with the examination of item 7 of the agenda: the report of the Digital, Innovation and Modern Technologies Committee on the Government's draft law on open data and the re-use of public sector information (prints 1338 and 1390). I ask Mrs Agnieszka Soin to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u275	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I have the honour to present a report to the Committee on Digitisation, Innovation and Modern Technologies from the first reading of the Government's draft law on open data and the re-use of public sector information, prints 1338 and 1390, which took place at the committee meeting on 13 July this year. The need for a law on open data and the re-use of public sector information arises from the need to implement Directive 2019/1024/EU of 20 June 2019 on open data and the re-use of public sector information. The proposed law will replace the current law of 25 February 2016 on the re-use of public sector information. The main objective of the proposed law is to strengthen the data-based market, among others. by increasing the amount of information made available by the public sector and providing users with access to valuable data resources made available through the application's programming interface and machine-readable formats. Such data distribution will be particularly important for professional users, e.g. programmers, IT companies. More data can also be used for analysis, research or artificial intelligence solutions, including for business and industry, or crisis modelling, e.g. In case of an outbreak. The Act will adapt Polish legislation to the requirements of the Open Data Directive. Within the framework of the proposed regulation, new categories of data were identified from public sector information, i.e. high-value data with significant benefits for society, the environment and the economy, and dynamic data, i.e. data subject to frequent real-time updates or updates, e.g. environmental data, data generated by factors, traffic data. For the two separate categories of information, specific rules were laid down for making such resources available for reuse. In order to increase the amount of public sector information available for re-use, the scope of the Act was also covered by data which were so far outside the re-use regulation. This applies to research data which will be subject to re-use free of charge if they are produced or collected in the context of a public scientific activity and are already made publicly available online, and data derived from so-called. public undertakings, i.e. public undertakings within the meaning of Article. 2 point 3 of the Open Data Directive, which operates in selected sectors of the economy, including: the transport, electricity, gas, water and postal services sector. The adaptation of Polish legislation to the requirements of the Open Data Directive is an essential objective of the proposed law. At the same time, however, solutions that go beyond the minimum laid down by the Directive will be introduced. The most important ones are the data portal. gov.pl, the data opening program and the network of data openness representatives. In addition, a comprehensive conceptual structure has been developed in the proposed law, introducing a legal glossary containing, among others,: definitions of open data and application programming interface. The use of standard licences has been allowed and a single review body has been introduced against decisions relating to the transmission of public sector information at the request issued at first instance by obliged entities. The role of the Board of Appeal, i.e. the second instance body will be provided by the Minister responsible for IT. The project met with a positive acceptance of the members of the committee. It was adopted in conjunction with amendments, of which editorial and legislative amendments prevail. On behalf of the Committee on Digitisation, Innovation and Modern Technology, I recommend that the High House adopt a law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u277	High Izbo! The fact that the modern data economy stands is a cliché. You have the tools to counter this, you have the regulatory tools, but you also have the tool that we are talking about today, i.e. a tool to which data are produced by the State, on behalf of the State, for public money. We know very well that today most international corporations make decisions on the basis of an analysis of data that has previously been processed in very detail. We also want to point out that, since the state is one of the largest repositories of data throughout the market, this data should be shared and encouraged, especially the market and entrepreneurs - including smaller entrepreneurs, not only huge companies - to use this data, analyse it on its basis and finally make business decisions. Referring to Mr Carrot's words, I will point out that particularly the development of artificial intelligence, the expansion of analytical tools to use this data, this is certainly a very important area, because data, as we say today, are a bit of the currency of the 21st century. It can also be said that they are ammunition for the development of artificial intelligence, a resource on the basis of which artificial intelligence can develop, learn, build appropriate mechanisms that will improve the functioning of the state and citizens. Therefore, when we talk about data policy in the context of the development of artificial intelligence and the construction of tools that these data process - when it comes to data opening, we want to ensure that this law not only increases data supply, but also their quality. The data supply itself, if it does not go with quality, can cause that data to not be used too willingly. We must therefore also build properly professional analytical tools that will draw appropriate conclusions from these data, also suitable for the state administration for subsequent implementation. Ladies and gentlemen! As regards the specific questions - I would like to ask Mr Tuduja - first of all, let us remember that we have a GDPR policy that protects people's data very clearly and shows the extent to which they are used. It is not that we can absolutely process any data in any way, regardless of the time frame and willingness to use it. ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u301	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! It concerns, among others, the following: removing barriers to the marketing of investment funds within the European Union and establishing clear rules for the withdrawal of investment funds from its activities. It enhances transparency, strengthens investor protection and facilitates access to information on applicable or advertising regulations. A central database shall also be entered, which shall be accessible via the website. The scope of the data which investment funds - and open funds and closed funds - must provide to supervisory authorities before the launch of the marketing of instruments within the European Union is extended. It also affects the rules on crowdfunding funding. About 2050 will support this project, assuming that it is our duty to implement European law into Polish law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u315	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Government's draft Law on Aid and Loan Funds from print No 1313 was the subject of first reading in the Committee on Public Finance on 20 July. The project didn't cause much controversy. Comments are mostly legislative proposals and ordering of the Legislative Offices of the Sejm. The activity of employee relief and loan banks, commonly known for years, has so far been regulated in the Trade Unions Act of 23 May 1991 and in the Decree of the Council of Ministers of 19 December 1992 on employee relief and loan banks and cooperative savings and credit banks in workplaces. As a result of the amendment of the Law on Trade Unions in force since 1 January 2019, which introduced the concept of employer and employee in place of the employer and the person carrying out the employment work, and as the issue of relief and lending banks is related to the activity of trade unions only indirectly through social control of trade unions over cash registers, it was decided to exclude from the Trade Unions Act provisions on relief and lending banks and to include them in a separate act. As a result of the solutions adopted in this Act in order to be a member of the relief and loan fund, you do not need to be a member of the trade unions, and credit unions may be created by an employer with whom there are no trade unions. The Act contains rules on the creation, organisation and operation of credit unions for employers and their liquidation. Specific provisions are to be included in the statutes of the relief and loan banks and the law specifies a whole range of those provisions which do not, however, form a closed catalogue. The employer can create 10 people working for the employer and not, as has been the case so far, 10 employees. As I mentioned, the difference is that the concept of a person doing a job is a broader concept than the concept of an employee, because it also includes, apart from those who earn a salary from the employment relationship, people like that. contractors or contractors of works, etc. The law also regulates the control over the activities of the relief and loan banks. All trade unions at the employer's place of control and, if there is no unanimity and agreement in this respect, the largest trade union. If there are no trade unions, which we also meet with, such control is exercised by the employee council. In the absence of a staff council, the control shall be exercised by the representation of the persons selected in accordance with the procedure adopted with the employer concerned. Thus, as I mentioned a moment ago, the control functions of trade unions vis-à-vis credit unions, e.g. by means of the right to convene a general meeting of the members of the cash register at the request of representatives of the trade union and to participate in it of the representative of the trade union. In addition, the draft contains provisions on compulsory and optional removal of members from the list of members of the relief and loan fund, lays down rules on the granting of loans and assistance, rules on the cancellation of a member of the board or of the audit committee before the end of the term of office and on the execution of supplementary elections, as well as on the payment of the costs of the inter-company relief and loan fund, and includes provisions on the payment of the Member's contribution, the adoption of a resolution on liquidation if the general meeting of the members makes such an initiative. The Act also contains provisions concerning the handling of data covered by GDPR, as well as an indication of the 18-month period for adapting the statutes to the provisions of the current Act - 18 months after its entry into force - and an 18-month period for notification to the national official register. Members' doubts during the committee's work raised the protection of personal data, particularly in this area, which concerns sensitive data related to health. The explanation from the Ministry is that health data will be required only if it is necessary to obtain aid in the form of loans or grants. A rather interesting proposal was also made to remove the assistance provided by the relief and loan funds from the execution. Various state institutions have submitted a lot of comments to the draft law - we have them in the protocol attached to the bill - both GIODO and Solvency and other trade unions, employers' representatives. I believe that not all the requests made by these bodies have been taken into account during the consultation, as is apparent from the information contained in the protocol. I think it would be worth relinquishing them, and some of these demands may still be taken into account at this stage of the procession. I would like to ask you, as rapporteur, for further examination of the draft law, which, as a single project on the activities of relief and lending banks, covers issues in a separate law as a whole. This will make it easier for those interested, often poorly moving in the legal field, to have access to the provisions of this law, which is used by many Poles. Many Poles have been benefiting from the benefits of the loan and relief funds for years. Thank you for your attention.

Aid-lending banks are a form of self-help, offering non-refundable benefits and low-interest loans to job providers, including pensioners and pensioners. Sometimes a loan from a relief and loan fund is the only way to obtain the necessary financial resources, free of additional costs in the form of commissions or interest, which is particularly important in difficult times, such as pandemics, as a healthy counterweight to pathologies lurking from everywhere on desperate people's moments or quick loans, pushing those in need only into subsequent loops of debt. Aid and loan funds are certainly not a remedy for tragically low wages in Poland, for tragically low pensions, for tragically low pensions, but are a form of self-help, a form of co-help. It is a good thing that such a pretty quality as camaraderie survived in this form in those bailouts that will now be governed by a separate law. This draft law was consulted socially and was also consulted by the National Cooperative Savings &amp; Credit Fund, which presented some proposals, some postulates. She received a letter from the Minister of Development, Work and Technology in November Ub. R. that all these comments have been taken into account. However, they have not been taken into account, and in principle one fundamental one has not been taken into account and came out at the next stage of the work on this project. The Left Parliamentary Club would therefore like to amend this proposed by the National Cooperative Savings and Credits Fund in its second reading. This amendment is to be based on the fact that after the article. 58 The following Article is inserted: 58a as follows: In the Act of 5 November 2009 on cooperative savings and credit banks in Article. In paragraph 10, paragraph 10 is replaced by the following: 2 - I will no longer read the entire paragraph - after the words "housing communities" the words "and "credit-support" are added. The point is that historically cooperative savings and credit banks were set up in the workplaces, as is the case with those of our employee relief and lending banks, and the basis for creating both the provisions of the Art. 39 Trade Unions Act. They were supervised by trade unions. At that time, cooperative funds could only be organised by natural persons. This principle was maintained in 1995, but the next SKOK Act allowed the possibility of the members of the cash register not only of natural persons but also of non-governmental organisations, cooperatives, trade unions or housing communities operating among members. Thus, while the first phase of cooperative banks' operations could not be concerned with keeping the bank's accounts, it does not seem that the maintenance of the requirement to hold a bank-only deposit-and-lending bank account by future cashiers is currently justified. Hence, this change opening up the possibility to keep the bank accounts also by savings and credit banks operating in workplaces, and not only by banks. The Left Parliamentary Club also welcomes the fact that welfare and loan funds can be created everywhere, in any establishment, including a private, small company, and can be founded by people who provide work not only on the basis of a contract of employment, but also on the basis of work contracts or contract-orders. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u349	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The Act on the revision of the Act on Radiophony and Television and the Act on Cinema, submitted to the High Sejm, is the implementation of Directive 2018/1808 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 14 November 2018 amending the previous Directive of 2010 on the coordination of certain provisions laid down by law, regulation and administrative action in Member States concerning the provision of audiovisual media services, called the Audiovisual Media Services Directive. The revision of European legislation has taken place in order to bring European and Member States' regulations into line with a rapidly growing and changing media market. According to the reasons for the implementation of the above Directive, the Polish legal order is to be amended by amending the provisions of the Act on Radiophony and Television, the Act of 29 December 1992. The amendment of this law sets out the rules and conditions for carrying out activities in the field of the provision of media services, as well as video-sharing platforms, the division of powers between State authorities in the field of regulation and supervision, the rights and obligations of media service providers and video-sharing platforms, as well as the rights of recipients of these services and the conditions for the pursuit of the public interest in radio and television. Most of the implementation changes have been introduced within the framework of the current structure of the Radio and Television Act, with certain changes, of course, resulting from the indications, from the recitals of the Directive. However, an important chapter on the subject was added. Video sharing platforms. The subject of the amendment includes, inter alia, jurisdiction over media service providers, as well as the competence and functioning of the National Radio and Television Council. Among other things, it follows. to extend its competence to activities in relation to video-sharing platforms. Further: the subject of the amendment includes the modification of statutory definitions, including: the definition of commercial communications, product sponsorship and placement, as well as information obligations of media service providers, including on the ownership structure of the media, in line with the very important wording of the directive, I quote: Transparency of media ownership is directly linked to freedom of expression, the foundation of democratic systems. Information on the ownership structure of media service providers, if such ownership results in control of the content of the services provided or significant impact on this content - and let us add that this is usually the case - allows users to consciously assess such content. In Polish practice we lacked implementation of this indication for many years. The subject of this amendment also includes commercial communications in media services, e.g. the permissible time limit for advertising and teleshopping, the protection of programmes and programmes for children, as well as for media services, the protection of consumers, including minors, against undesirable content affecting their physical and mental development or threatening to promote attitudes that are not socially acceptable, such as racism, xenophobia and others. It also includes the promotion of European creativity in media services by requiring a specific participation of European broadcasts in television programmes, the so-called. European quota - in an audiovisual service at the request of 30% of the catalogue are to be European works, which is now 20%. Further: the subject of the regulation includes facilities for persons with disabilities so that, under the directive, media services become, with proportionate measures and continuously more and more accessible to persons with disabilities, as well as the regulation of video-sharing platforms, which so far have not been properly regulated and which are increasingly competing, increasingly on the market with the same recipients and the same financial revenues as audiovisual media services and have a strong impact on the public. The provisions of the Directive are probably rational in the assessment of all Members, because they were indeed in agreement with the committee and were exceptionally not controversial. Therefore, their implementation into the legal order, first prepared by the subcommittee, later by the committee, was adopted unanimously, as evidenced by the outcome of the vote in the Committee on Culture and Communications. The requirements of the European Directive are reflected in the draft in a rational manner, meeting the requirements of the Directive and maintaining (The Bell) the autonomous assessment of Polish media realities and standards of social expectations. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will vote in favour of the draft law amending the Act on Radiophony and Television and the Act on Cinema submitted to the High Sejm by the Committee on Culture and Media. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u355	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish Coalition - Polish People's Party, the Union of European Democrats and Conservatives, I have the honour to present the position of the club on the Act amending the Act on Radiophony and Television and the Act on Cinema, print No. 1340. This project implements into Polish law the European Parliament Directive of 14 November 2018 amending the European Union Directive on the coordination of certain laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States concerning the provision of audiovisual media services due to developments in the media market. The most important assumptions of the bill are the introduction of regulations on the operation of video-sharing platforms. The draft law, following the directive, extends the scope of regulation for video-sharing platforms. This amendment modifies the rules for placing commercial communications in media services. This change will modify consumer protection regulations, in particular for minors, against harmful content. The broadcasters and suppliers will have to inform the audience in each programme about the content that these broadcasts contain, and so parents will be able to make informed decisions regarding the choice of content that children view. This amendment also modifies the principles of promoting and promoting European creativity by media service providers - from 20% to 30%. It also modifies rules on the use of facilities for persons with disabilities in audiovisual media services. The draft law requires audiovisual service providers to ensure that at least 30% The programmes placed in the catalogue included amenities for people with disabilities. The amount of facilities will be gradually increased from 5% in 2022 to 30% in 2025. It also amended the information obligations of media service providers. Consequently, the draft law requires broadcasters, media service providers and platform providers to make available on the website of the entity information on the persons managing the entity and information on the ownership structure. In view of the above and the recommendation of the subcommittee, which was created in order to examine in detail the changes to this project, I recommend that the High House adopt this amendment and inform you that the Polish Coalition - Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats and Conservatives will vote in favour of this amendment. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u371	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to remind you, because we talked about this at the committee meeting, that the law implementing the European Union directive is so designed that we do not go beyond what is required by the EU directive. We have tried not to include in this law any additional things other than those arising from the directive itself. This law has been consulted for almost a year, because since September Ub. r., with the market, with public media, with commercial media, with VOD platforms, with regulatory authorities of the type National Radio and Television Council or the National Media Council, and it seems to us that such a common sense consensus has been built in relation to the solutions that we propose in this law. I think it is, too, because we see it in parliamentary work. At both the committee meeting - twice already - and at the meeting of the specially appointed subcommittee, this law did not raise much controversy. The consensus around the issues needed to regulate is clearly felt, because indeed since we have the Radio and Television Act, the cinema bill, it has passed: in the first case - more than 25 years, in the second case - 16 years, and the market is changing and some things need to be adapted to the changed market. This was also the intention of the directive to introduce these changes throughout the European Union in view of market developments. Mr Gawkowski has already left, but I would like to say that his accusation is absolutely wrong, because I will remind you - in a moment's time I will develop - that all these dilemmas and doubts about interrupting the broadcasts are not about public television. I'm very keen on that. No film or children's broadcast may be interrupted by advertising on public television, and such a charge was formulated by Mr Gawkowski. We are talking about some changes, but only with regard to commercial media. And I'm going to say that right away, because there's been a lot of comments about interrupting a movie that's a children's show and advertising. According to the Directive, I will quote the text, this is the proposal: The issue of children's broadcasts can only be interrupted by television advertising once in the course of each scheduled programme period of no less than 30 minutes, provided that the duration of the programme provided for in the programme is longer than 30 minutes. This is the proposal contained in the Directive. However, we are not going that far, and I would like to emphasise that very strongly. The draft law prepared by us only partially implements the provision of this directive and introduces the possibility of interrupting certain programmes for children in the Polish order, but it is a more limited provision than the directive implies. The directive allows the interruption of children's programmes lasting more than 30 minutes, and we propose that only films lasting more than 60 minutes be interrupted. To be honest, who knows the market, knows that children's programmes generally do not last more than an hour. If something lasts longer, it's Pixar's movies, those big Hollywood movies that have the dimension of movies. They can be treated as programmes for children, but they can also be treated as family cinema simply, family cinema, which has a commercial dimension. In this case, we will actually allow commercial publishers to interrupt advertising with such a family film, so let's call it, after 60 minutes - not after 30 minutes, but after 60 minutes - because it raises the question whether these great movies of Pixar, cinema, American, are programmes for children or is it a family cinema. I think it's more like a family movie, and these movies are gonna be interrupted. There are usually no children's programmes lasting more than 60 minutes. And this is, of course, related to the need for broadcasters to bear the costs of obtaining such films. We want Polish broadcasters to have no worse situation than European broadcasters so that they also have the opportunity to acquire such programmes on the market and are not in a worse situation because they cannot broadcast advertising after 60 minutes. The second set of comments concerned the independence of the National Radio and Television Council. This independence is guaranteed in the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. The National Council is a constitutional body, which is rare in European legislation, and we are a constitutional body, and this independence is guaranteed both by the Constitution and by the existing Radio and Television Act. The amendment only concerns the fact that so far, for more than a quarter of a century, the term of office of the National Radio and Television Council in the current composition of its members may be interrupted if the Sejm, the Senate, fails to accept the report and the President's failure to confirm it. The provisions of the directive required that this failure to accept reports should be justified in order to justify such a political decision by the Sejm or the Senate, and that is what we are introducing. If the Sejm decides to cancel the members of the National Council, it must be justified not to adopt the report - the same as the Senate. [[And the president?]] The President is relieved of his obligation to state his reasons... [[Why?]] ... because in accordance with Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council concerning the implementation of Council Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the In no case shall the Constitution provide for any provision of the obligation to justify the provision. [[Mr Iwona Śledzińska-Katarasinska: Isn't it allowed?]] So we cannot make an exception in this case, because it would be contrary to the Constitution of the Republic of Poland. [[Member Iwona Śledzińska-Katarasińska: No, not with a constitution.]] If such resolutions are adopted by the Sejm and the Senate, they must justify it, while the President, in the procedure of confirming any of these resolutions of the Sejm and the Senate, does not have to provide such justification. As far as the comments on sport are concerned, mainly Mr Zimoch was active in the committee on this matter, but also other Members in the stand have taken this issue, we are indeed introducing the principle that it is permissible to interrupt the broadcasting of sports competitions in order to give a single advertisement. The directive states, I quote: Single television advertising and teleshopping spots are acceptable during sporting events. Single television advertising and teleshopping spots, other than those placed during the broadcast of sports events, must be an exception. The proposed article takes into account the provision of this Directive, as well as the proposals made by the market and the National Radio and Television Council in the course of a public consultation. The aim is to enable broadcasters to finance expensive - we all know - sports broadcasting rights and equalise the competitive conditions of Polish broadcasters vis-à-vis foreign competition. It should be noted that many television programmes offering access to sports broadcasts in Poland fall under the jurisdiction of another Member State, e.g. popular in Poland Eurosport. He would have the right, and our broadcasters wouldn't have the right. Many of them already on the basis of the existing regulations adopted in these Member States significantly more frequently interrupt sports broadcasts, also in order to give a single advertising spot. The purpose of this provision is to allow broadcasters to place individual advertising spots e.g. in natural breaks in competitions that do not come from the rules on playing out the competitions concerned - e.g. break during the ski jump competition due to bad weather - or during breaks placed in the transmission by the entity from which the sender acquires the right to issue it, e.g. in the case of several hours of cycling races, the operators preparing the transmission shall introduce several-second breaks so that the sender can put in advertising at that time. Furthermore, thanks to the introduced regulation, it will be possible to broadcast an advertising spot on a part of the screen during a sports broadcast. This is also technically possible now: we watch sports broadcasts all the time, and some advertising is on part of the screen. As far as jurisdiction over foreign entities is concerned, the regulation we propose concerns this issue to a limited extent, only vis-à-vis broadcasters and VOD providers and only in terms of the obligation to charge to the Polish Film Institute. We are looking for balance here, because these broadcasters draw revenue in the territory of the Republic of Poland and should pay fees to the Polish Institute of Film Art, like everyone else who uses the Polish market, also have some connection with the film industry and should support Polish cinema. So that this regulation only applies to this. As regards the very detailed questions: Mr Szopiński from the Left and Mrs Nowogórska from the Polish Coalition, we will answer them in writing in accordance with my wish and my suggestion. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u399	Thank you very much, Marshal. I'm sorry, but it turns out five minutes isn't enough, given the crowd. You're welcome, my fellow Member. Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Volumes were written about the Solidarity that was born in 1980. A couple of Ph.D.'s were probably protected, a few habilitations were done. The history of this movement, its fate, its meaning for Poland are actually well developed and converted. However, there is a passage in this story that I still think has not been appreciated. This is the role of women in the movement of Solidarity. I am convinced that both at a time when Solidarity was born and when Solidarity was pushed underground, especially at a time of war, but also at a time when it was reborn, the considerable burden of all duties was assumed by women of Solidarity. There are studies that show the characters of this movement, there is a reportage, there is one documentary, it is true, but it still does not reflect the scale and strength of their participation and effort. Therefore, at a time when a very significant anniversary is coming - the 40th anniversary of the hunger march, whose apogee took place in Łódź, the march, which was mainly the march of women, the women were headed by it, the women with children in wheelchairs - we thought it was a good opportunity to expand it a little and make this march a symbol of the importance and role of women. And so was the draft resolution on honoring the role of women in the solidarity revolution of the 1980s. That was the original title of this resolution. Today, the Committee on Culture and Media worked on this project, and I have the honour to be the rapporteur. I will start by thanking you, because ultimately the resolution was adopted in committee actually by acclamation, because there was no objection, even there was no need to vote on the whole resolution. That's what I'm happy about, and that's what these women really deserve. This does not mean that there was no discussion at the meeting, not even a very hot discussion and exchange of views, perhaps even light arguments or disputes, but, as I said, we finally adopted this resolution in absolute agreement. The first amendment that was made was to change the title of the resolution. In the document, I believe, there will be a new title, although the printing is still old. I'll read the new title because it's important. It is a resolution on honoring the role of women in the solidarity revolution on the 40th anniversary of hunger marches. It was a suggestion of parts of colleagues, as the pretext for preparing this resolution became starving marches, which in July 1981 took over without a small whole Poland. So in the title is a reminder that this is a resolution on the 40th anniversary of hunger marches. In the intention of the authors and applicants of this resolution it was to be a symbol of the resolution. It wasn't supposed to be part of a story, it wasn't supposed to be a section of the movement, it wasn't just supposed to appreciate the role of Łódź or the Łódź region. This was supposed to be a symbol, it was to pay tribute to all women who over the years were brave, were heroes, performed both in the union movement and in the private life of their husbands, fathers, brothers, especially in the martial law, who were publishing books, which were editing newspapers that kept those who were hiding from the esbecies of the leading activists, who were always helping, who worked in interned aid centers. There were women everywhere and around. And it's for them that resolution, and it's about them that resolution. As I said, a new version of the title was adopted. It's about two elements. I'm not speaking as an applicant anymore, I'm speaking as a committee rapporteur. The two elements aroused some controversy, but they were managed to eliminate it. The first is that in this resolution, there are still Łódź and the Łódź region, and there was the idea that not only did this march take place there, but also in other cities. Yes, it is. Only when they tried to add these cities through amendments, it turned out that if there were three such, there would be three others, and if there were five, then the essence of this topic would die at all. In total, consensus was reached and it was found that the center of these hunger marches became Łódź and many other cities of the Łódź region, and it was probably possible to stop there. The second point is, I would say, even more difficult and may have aroused more discussion, but we also managed to communicate in committee work. It's a matter of... Here are the symbolic names, such names of activists of Solidarity, which are obvious to everyone. It's so that a primary school student who doesn't remember at all back then knows who we're talking about, what kind of name that is. At first it was said that maybe these names were not enough, too many were asked why not alphabetically or perhaps yes or otherwise. These are symbols and their names are symbolic. These are historical figures, and these are figures from different wings, currents, directions of Solidarity. Here are the names of the ladies who are alive, some of them still work, but others that are already gone. And there are names of such ladies that I don't know if they'd sit next to each other today. I don't know, it's not important, but then they acted under the exact same idea of freedom and independence, improvement of existence and dignity. These were the elements women fought for, so we adopted this concept. It is not, as I repeat, a full list, supplemented by about five more names. It's hard to argue. It's important that we don't think those are names... Why doesn't he have one? Why doesn't he have one? No, it's a symbol, it's a monument, it's a memorial board. This resolution is meant to be a symbol. And if so, I hope that during the vote the Sejm will adopt this resolution as unanimously as the committee did. If you don't mind, I'll read it because that's what I'm saying, and actually, ladies and gentlemen, you can't really know what it is. The resolution of the Polish Sejm on honoring the role of women in the solidarity revolution on the 40th anniversary of hunger marches. July 1981 was a month of street protests called starvation marches of desperate residents of Polish cities and towns. Coordinated by the NSZZ 'Solidarity', the wave of rebellion against increases in food prices, the reduction of card rations of meat and its preparations and the destabilizing shelves took over the whole of Poland. The center of hunger marches became Łódź and many other cities of the Łódź region, and their undisputed leaders turned out to be women from this region. On July 25, 5,000 protested, but on July 30, Piotrkowska Street filled a tens of thousands of people demanding 'bread and freedom'. It was led by women with children in wheelchairs led by the march organizer - Janina Końzak, a worker of the Łódź Rubber Industry Plant 'Stomil'. Interned during the martial law and then forced to emigrate Janina Końzak is one of the thousands of women 'Solidarity' who co-created this movement and then, despite harassment and persecution, co-organized its underground activities. Women whose heroism and determination we owe today to free Poland. Anna Valentynowicz was born in Gdańsk Shipyard 'Solidarity' due to the exemption from crane work. Starting with it, you can create a name by name, a list of prominent activists 'Solidarity', about which history has no right to forget: Zofia Romaszewska, Zofia Kuratowska, Joanna Duda-Star, Alina Pienkowska, Bożena Rybicka, Barbara Labuda, Ewa Kubasiewicz, Helena Łuczywo, Henryk Krzywonos, Marta Modzelewska, Ewa Ossowska, Maryla Płońska, Jolanta Szczypińska, Grażyna Staniszewska... On the eve of the 40th anniversary of the Łódź hunger march, the biggest manifestation of this kind in the era of real socialism, the Polish Parliament expresses its highest appreciation to thousands of Poles and their leaders, paying tribute to their sacrifice, remembering that they have always been and are ready to fight for bread, freedom and dignity. That is the text of this resolution. In fulfilling the responsibility of the rapporteur, I will simply say that there is a mistake here, a typo - we apologize, but at such a rapid pace it was going on, the committee meeting was just now - not Marta Modzelewska and Magda Modzelewska. This (the bell) will be corrected in the Seym materials. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-07-21-sejm-35-1.u403	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, In July 2021, as we have heard, the 40th anniversary of hunger marches in Poland. Coordinated by the unionists of NSZZ.Solidarity was an expression of the protest of citizens against limited access to food supply in the times of the growing economic crisis of the country, a clear proof of the failure of the economic policy of the communist authorities and the failure of the system of real socialism. Thus, as one of many elements of the events of the 1980s, they contributed to the overthrow of the communist regime in Poland. The reason for starvation marches in 1981 was insufficient food supply for shops, including regulated ones, a reduction in postcard meat allocations and an increase in food prices. The demonstration on this background took place in July 1981 in many cities of Poland, among others. in Czestochowa, Cracow, Nowy Sącz, Puławy, Przemyśl, Szczecin. The largest number of speeches took place in the Łódź Voivodeship, where starvation marches passed through streets, among others. Bełchatowa, Kutna, Łask, Piotrków Trybunalski, Tomaszów Mazowiecki, Zduńska Wola and above all, mostly Łódź. It was in Łódź that on 25 July the first hunger march took place with the participation of approx. 5 thousand. The march of 30 July 1981 took place in Łódź, which gathered from 50 to 100,000 according to various estimates. people and is considered to be the biggest street demonstration of the day of the Polish People's Republic. Its participants were mainly women with children, and organizer Janina Kończak, social and trade union activist, worker of the Łódź Rubber Industry Departments (Stomil), interned during the martial law, which later, under pressure from the authorities, left for France, without continuing to emigrate efforts to support pro-democratic movements in Poland and help the country. In March 2021, she was awarded the Cross of Freedom and Solidarity. Ladies and gentlemen! On the 40th anniversary of the hunger march in Łódź, the biggest demonstration of the street day of the Polish People's Republic, the Sejm of the Republic of Poland has the chance to pay tribute to the sacrifice of Łódź women and their determination to fight for a decent life and to express special appreciation for Janina Końzak's life path and her contribution to the fight against the communist regime. He also has the opportunity to express his highest appreciation for thousands of Poles and their leaders fighting for free Poland in those years, among them for Anna Valentynowicz, but also for many, many other outstanding women, who - as we can read in the draft resolution (The Bell) of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland - were always and are ready to fight for bread, freedom and dignity.
